================================================
FILE: rust-toolchain.toml
================================================
[toolchain]
# Pin an exact stable toolchain so clippy/rustfmt behave identically on every
# developer machine and in CI. This is what actually eliminates local/CI lint
# drift: the floating `stable` alias silently rolls forward (e.g. 1.95 -> 1.96
# adds new default-warn lints), so one machine fails `-D warnings` on lints
# another never sees. rustup auto-installs this exact version in the repo, and a
# developer can still opt into a newer toolchain ad hoc via `cargo +<toolchain>`.
#
# This is separate from, and complementary to, the MSRV floor in `rust-version`
# (root Cargo.toml `[workspace.package]`): the pin says "the toolchain we all
# run"; the floor says "the minimum we compile on." Bump this pin deliberately
# (and keep it >= the floor) rather than tracking `stable` and inheriting drift.
channel = "1.98.0"
components = ["clippy", "rustfmt"]
targets = ["aarch64-unknown-linux-musl"]

FILE: ci/scripts/run-shell-tests.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
# Run every self-contained shell regression test discovered in the repository.
set -euo pipefail

SCRIPT_DIR="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")" && pwd)"
REPO_ROOT="$(cd "${SCRIPT_DIR}/../.." && pwd)"

cd "${REPO_ROOT}"

test_dirs=(ci/scripts scripts/tests)
if [ -d .buildkite/scripts ]; then
    test_dirs+=(.buildkite/scripts)
fi

mapfile -t tests < <(
    find "${test_dirs[@]}" \
        -maxdepth 1 \
        -type f \
        -name 'test-*.sh' \
        -print \
        | sed 's#^\./##' \
        | sort
)

if [ "${#tests[@]}" -eq 0 ]; then
    echo "No shell regression tests discovered." >&2
    exit 1
fi

printf '%s\n' "--- :shell: Running ${#tests[@]} discovered shell regression tests"
for test_file in "${tests[@]}"; do
    echo "+++ ${test_file}"
    bash "${test_file}"
done



================================================
FILE: ci/scripts/test-setup-nextest-postgres.sh
================================================
#!/usr/bin/env bash
set -euo pipefail

repo_root="$(cd "$(dirname "${BASH_SOURCE[0]}")/../.." && pwd)"
cd "${repo_root}"

tmp_dir="$(mktemp -d)"
trap 'rm -rf "${tmp_dir}"' EXIT

fake_bin="${tmp_dir}/bin"
mkdir -p "${fake_bin}"
cat >"${fake_bin}/docker" <<'SH'
#!/usr/bin/env bash
if [ "${1:-}" = run ]; then
    echo "simulated unavailable Docker daemon" >&2
    exit 1
fi
echo "unexpected docker command: $*" >&2
exit 99
SH
chmod +x "${fake_bin}/docker"

run_setup() {
    local env_file="$1"
    PATH="${fake_bin}:${PATH}" \
        NEXTEST_ENV="${env_file}" \
        NEXTEST_RUN_ID="review-test" \
        bash ci/scripts/setup-nextest-postgres.sh
}

env_file="${tmp_dir}/nextest.env"
: >"${env_file}"
if run_setup "${env_file}"; then
    echo "FAIL: setup succeeded with unavailable Docker" >&2
    exit 1
fi
if [ -s "${env_file}" ]; then
    echo "FAIL: failed setup wrote a partial nextest environment" >&2
    exit 1
fi

echo "nextest Postgres setup failure test passed"

FILE: crates/apps/builder/cli/src/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn build_flags_default_to_publish_enabled() {
    let cli = Cli::parse_from([
        "space-buildmgr",
        "build",
        "--recipe",
        "/tmp/r.toml",
        "--id",
        "seed",
    ]);
    let Commands::Build { publish_tags, .. } = cli.command else {
        panic!("expected build command");
    };
    assert!(publish_tags.is_empty());
}

#[test]
fn build_flags_allow_explicit_publish_tags() {
    let cli = Cli::parse_from([
        "space-buildmgr",
        "build",
        "--recipe",
        "/tmp/r.toml",
        "--id",
        "seed",
        "--publish-tag",
        "latest",
        "--publish-tag",
        "prod",
    ]);
    let Commands::Build { publish_tags, .. } = cli.command else {
        panic!("expected build command");
    };
    assert_eq!(publish_tags, vec!["latest", "prod"]);
}

FILE: crates/apps/db/migrator/src/main.rs
================================================
use clap::Parser;
use pplx_space_db_migrate::{Database, DbMigrateRunConfig, SERVICE_NAME};

#[derive(Parser)]
#[command(
    name = "space-db-migrator",
    about = "SPACE SQLx migration runner",
    version,
    disable_version_flag = true
)]
struct Cli {
    /// Print version.
    #[arg(short = 'v', long = "version", action = clap::ArgAction::Version)]
    _version: (),

    #[command(subcommand)]
    database: Database,
}

impl From<Cli> for DbMigrateRunConfig {
    fn from(cli: Cli) -> Self {
        Self {
            database: cli.database,
        }
    }
}

#[tokio::main]
async fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    pplx_space_common_cli::init_logging_with_startup_banner(SERVICE_NAME);
    let cli = Cli::parse();

    Ok(pplx_space_db_migrate::run(cli.into()).await?)
}



================================================
FILE: crates/apps/local/node-mgr/svc/src/config.rs
================================================
//! Runtime configuration for the local (non-Firecracker) node-manager.

use std::path::PathBuf;

use pplx_space_common_svc::core::host::linux::paths::NODE_MANAGER_SOCKET;

/// Fallback seed rootfs image used when `PrepareSandbox` does not carry a usable
/// `template_dir`. Baked into the runtime's image at build time.
pub const DEFAULT_SEED_ROOTFS: &str = "/space/templates/seed/rootfs.ext4";

/// Effective configuration resolved from CLI args + environment.
#[derive(Clone, Debug)]
pub struct Config {
    /// node-manager gRPC Unix socket to bind.
    pub socket: PathBuf,
    /// Seed rootfs image used when no template build dir is available.
    pub seed_rootfs: PathBuf,
}

impl Config {
    /// Build the config from the socket override and the environment.
    pub fn resolve(socket: Option<PathBuf>) -> Self {
        Self {
            socket: socket.unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(NODE_MANAGER_SOCKET)),
            seed_rootfs: PathBuf::from(DEFAULT_SEED_ROOTFS),
        }
    }
}



================================================
FILE: crates/apps/local/node-mgr/svc/src/lib.rs
================================================
//! Local (non-Firecracker) node-manager runtime for the kind smoke harness.
//!
//! Implements `space.node.v1.NodeManager` with guest-as-`systemd-nspawn`,
//! reflink rootfs cloning, synthetic networking, file-based snapshots, and
//! btrfs RPCs that decline to force the `Full` ext4 codec — so sandbox-manager,
//! node-api, volume-manager, and the spaced bridge run unchanged on a laptop or
//! CI box with no KVM, no btrfs, and no `NET_ADMIN`.

pub mod config;
pub mod paths;
pub mod process;
pub mod service;
pub mod snapshot;
pub mod state;

pub use config::Config;
pub use service::LocalNodeManager;

/// Per-sandbox guest (nspawn) log file, under the sandbox run dir.
pub const NSPAWN_LOG: &str = "nspawn.log";
/// Per-sandbox spaced-host log file, under the sandbox run dir.
pub const SPACED_HOST_LOG: &str = "spaced-host.log";

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;

use pplx_space_net_gateway::tls_sni::{ClientHelloScan, scan_client_hello};

use super::*;

/// The builder is only useful if the gateway's own parser agrees, so assert
/// against the parser rather than against a byte snapshot.
#[test]
fn the_gateway_parser_extracts_the_server_name_we_encoded() {
    let scan = scan_client_hello(&with_server_name("bench.example.com"));
    assert_eq!(
        scan.server_name().map(|name| name.as_str().to_string()),
        Some("bench.example.com".to_string())
    );
}

#[test]
fn a_hello_without_sni_carries_no_server_name() {
    assert_eq!(
        scan_client_hello(&without_server_name()),
        ClientHelloScan::NoServerName
    );
}



================================================
FILE: crates/apps/net/gateway/bench/src/wire/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;

#[test]
fn every_op_round_trips_through_the_header() {
    for op in [Op::Echo, Op::Sink, Op::Source] {
        let arg = 1u64 << 40;
        assert_eq!(decode(&encode(op, arg)), Some((op, arg)));
    }
}

#[test]
fn an_unknown_op_code_does_not_decode() {
    let mut header = encode(Op::Echo, 0);
    if let Some(code) = header.first_mut() {
        *code = 0xff;
    }
    assert_eq!(decode(&header), None);
}

FILE: crates/apps/net/gateway/svc/tests/cli.rs
================================================
use std::path::PathBuf;
use std::process::Command;

fn gateway_binary() -> PathBuf {
    std::env::var_os("NEXTEST_BIN_EXE_space-netgtw-svc")
        .map(PathBuf::from)
        .unwrap_or_else(|| PathBuf::from(env!("CARGO_BIN_EXE_space-netgtw-svc")))
}

#[test]
fn retired_tls_interception_mode_is_rejected() {
    let output = Command::new(gateway_binary())
        .args(["--tls-interception-mode", "off", "--version"])
        .output()
        .expect("run space-netgtw-svc");
    let stderr = String::from_utf8_lossy(&output.stderr);

    assert!(!output.status.success(), "retired flag was accepted");
    assert!(
        stderr.contains("unexpected argument '--tls-interception-mode'"),
        "unexpected stderr: {stderr}"
    );
}

FILE: crates/apps/ssh/cli/src/tests.rs
================================================
use super::openssh_set_env_value;

#[test]
fn openssh_set_env_value_leaves_simple_values_unquoted() {
    assert_eq!(
        openssh_set_env_value("abc_123:/bin").as_deref(),
        Some("abc_123:/bin")
    );
}

#[test]
fn openssh_set_env_value_quotes_spaces_and_escapes_specials() {
    assert_eq!(
        openssh_set_env_value("hello world").as_deref(),
        Some("\"hello world\"")
    );
    assert_eq!(
        openssh_set_env_value("a\"b\\c").as_deref(),
        Some("\"a\\\"b\\\\c\"")
    );
}

#[test]
fn openssh_set_env_value_rejects_line_breaks() {
    assert_eq!(openssh_set_env_value("line 1\nline 2"), None);
    assert_eq!(openssh_set_env_value("line 1\r\nline 2"), None);
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/public_store.rs

use std::str::FromStr;

#[derive(Debug)]
pub(crate) struct InvalidStoredEnum {
    pub(crate) field: &'static str,
    pub(crate) value: String,
}

pub(crate) fn parse_stored_enum<T>(field: &'static str, value: &str) -> Result<T, InvalidStoredEnum>
where
    T: FromStr,
{
    value.parse::<T>().map_err(|_| InvalidStoredEnum {
        field,
        value: value.to_string(),
    })
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/controllers/test_fixtures.rs

use pplx_space_common_core::{
    sql,
    types::{TemplateId, TemplateVisibility},
};
use pplx_space_db_test_fixtures::TestDbResult;
use sqlx::PgPool;
use uuid::Uuid;

pub(super) async fn insert_org_specific_template(
    pool: &PgPool,
    name: &str,
    owner_org: Uuid,
) -> TestDbResult<()> {
    sqlx::query(sql!(
        "INSERT INTO templates (id, name, description, org_id, visibility, network_policy_generation)
         VALUES ($1, $2, NULL, $3, $4, 1)"
    ))
    .bind(TemplateId::new().as_uuid())
    .bind(name)
    .bind(owner_org)
    .bind(TemplateVisibility::OrgSpecific.as_str())
    .execute(pool)
    .await?;
    Ok(())
}



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/api/src/controllers/sandbox/queries/create_env_tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;

#[test]
fn sanitize_create_env_keeps_valid_entries_and_drops_invalid_entries() {
    let env = BTreeMap::from([
        ("VALID".to_string(), "ok".to_string()),
        ("MULTILINE".to_string(), "line 1\nline 2".to_string()),
        ("BAD-NAME".to_string(), "drop".to_string()),
        ("BAD_VALUE".to_string(), "drop\0me".to_string()),
    ]);

    let sanitized = sanitize_create_env(&env);

    assert_eq!(sanitized.get("VALID").map(String::as_str), Some("ok"));
    assert_eq!(
        sanitized.get("MULTILINE").map(String::as_str),
        Some("line 1\nline 2")
    );
    assert!(!sanitized.contains_key("BAD-NAME"));
    assert!(!sanitized.contains_key("BAD_VALUE"));
}



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/api/src/controllers/substrate/pool_eligibility_tests.rs
================================================
use super::pool_eligible_with_env;

#[test]
fn env_bearing_create_is_ineligible_only_when_substrate_cannot_apply_env() {
    // Substrate-ineligible stays ineligible regardless of env.
    assert!(!pool_eligible_with_env(false, true, false));
    assert!(!pool_eligible_with_env(false, false, true));
    // Eligible + no env → eligible on any substrate.
    assert!(pool_eligible_with_env(true, true, false));
    assert!(pool_eligible_with_env(true, false, false));
    // Eligible + env → only when the substrate can apply env at adoption.
    assert!(pool_eligible_with_env(true, true, true));
    assert!(!pool_eligible_with_env(true, false, true));
}

pub use pplx_space_common_svc::federated_identity::{
    DEFAULT_FEDERATED_IDENTITY_ISSUER, FederatedIdentityPolicy, FederatedIdentityPolicyError,
};

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Eq, PartialEq)]
pub struct FederatedIdentityConfig {
    pub enabled: bool,
    pub policy: FederatedIdentityPolicy,
}

impl FederatedIdentityConfig {
    pub fn from_env() -> Result<Self, FederatedIdentityPolicyError> {
        Ok(Self {
            enabled: cfg!(feature = "federated-identity"),
            policy: FederatedIdentityPolicy::from_env()?,
        })
    }

    pub fn disabled() -> Self {
        Self {
            enabled: false,
            policy: FederatedIdentityPolicy::default(),
        }
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;

use super::*;

#[test]
fn feature_gate_controls_enabled_mode() {
    let config = FederatedIdentityConfig::from_env().unwrap();

    assert_eq!(config.enabled, cfg!(feature = "federated-identity"));
    assert_eq!(
        config.policy.issuer.as_str(),
        DEFAULT_FEDERATED_IDENTITY_ISSUER
    );
}

#[test]
fn disabled_config_uses_default_issuer() {
    let config = FederatedIdentityConfig::disabled();

    assert!(!config.enabled);
    assert_eq!(
        config.policy.issuer.as_str(),
        DEFAULT_FEDERATED_IDENTITY_ISSUER
    );
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/middleware/auth/tests.rs

use axum::http::{HeaderMap, HeaderValue};

use super::{api_key, bearer_token};

#[test]
fn extracts_bearer_token_from_authorization_header() {
    let mut headers = HeaderMap::new();
    headers.insert(
        "authorization",
        HeaderValue::from_static("Bearer session-token"),
    );

    assert_eq!(bearer_token(&headers), Some("session-token"));
}

#[test]
fn rejects_non_bearer_authorization_header() {
    let mut headers = HeaderMap::new();
    headers.insert("authorization", HeaderValue::from_static("Basic abc123"));

    assert_eq!(bearer_token(&headers), None);
}

#[test]
fn extracts_api_key_from_x_api_key_header() {
    let mut headers = HeaderMap::new();
    headers.insert("x-api-key", HeaderValue::from_static("e2b_test_key"));

    assert_eq!(api_key(&headers), Some("e2b_test_key"));
}

#[test]
fn rejects_empty_api_key_header() {
    let mut headers = HeaderMap::new();
    headers.insert("x-api-key", HeaderValue::from_static(""));

    assert_eq!(api_key(&headers), None);
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/routes/tests.rs

use axum::http::StatusCode;

use super::readyz_response;

#[test]
fn readyz_fails_when_draining() {
    let response = readyz_response(true, true);

    assert_eq!(response, (StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, "draining"));
}

#[test]
fn readyz_fails_until_reflector_is_ready() {
    let response = readyz_response(false, false);

    assert_eq!(
        response,
        (StatusCode::SERVICE_UNAVAILABLE, "pod reflector warming")
    );
}

#[test]
fn readyz_succeeds_when_reflector_is_ready_and_not_draining() {
    let response = readyz_response(true, false);

    assert_eq!(response, (StatusCode::OK, "ok"));
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/routes/debug/sources/mod.rs

use pplx_space_common_core::sql;
use sqlx::{PgPool, Postgres, Transaction};

pub(super) mod lifecycle;
pub(super) mod observed;
pub(super) mod pods;
pub(super) mod ports;
pub(super) mod snapshots;
pub(super) mod timeline;

pub(in crate::routes::debug) async fn begin_read_only(
    pool: &PgPool,
) -> Result<Transaction<'_, Postgres>, sqlx::Error> {
    let mut tx = pool.begin().await?;
    sqlx::query(sql!("SET TRANSACTION READ ONLY"))
        .execute(&mut *tx)
        .await?;
    Ok(tx)
}

pub(in crate::routes::debug) fn source_error(error: impl std::fmt::Display) -> String {
    error.to_string()
}

use super::*;
pub(crate) use crate::e2b::{build_sandbox_response, ecw_token};

pub(super) async fn require_ecw_api_host(
    State(ecw_hosts): State<EcwHostConfig>,
    request: Request,
    next: Next,
) -> Response {
    if ecw_hosts.api_pair_for_headers(request.headers()).is_some() {
        return next.run(request).await;
    }

    E2bApiError::not_found("Endpoint").into_response()
}

pub(super) const SPACE_ECW_HOSTS_ENV: &str = "SPACE_ECW_HOSTS";

// `ECW_DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_ID` is used when the E2B request omits `templateID`.
// `ECW_DEFAULT_CREATE_TIMEOUT_SECS` follows E2B's SDK create-time sandbox
// timeout default.
// `ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_*` values are only used when SPACE data is
// unavailable while shaping E2B responses.
pub(super) const ECW_DEFAULT_TEMPLATE_ID: &str = "seed";
pub(super) const ECW_DEFAULT_CREATE_TIMEOUT_SECS: u32 = 300;
pub(super) const ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_CPU_COUNT: u32 = 2;
pub(super) const ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_MEMORY_MB: u32 = 1024;
pub(super) const ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_DISK_SIZE_MB: u32 = 0;
pub(super) const ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_TIMEOUT_SECS: u32 = 15;

// E2B SDK gates filesystem upload behavior on semantic envd versions.
pub(super) use crate::e2b::ECW_RESPONSE_DEFAULT_ENVD_VERSION;

use pplx_space_common_core::types::SandboxId;

pub(super) const ECW_SANDBOX_API_LOG_EVENT: &str = "ecw_sandbox_api_request";
pub(super) const ECW_SANDBOX_ID_SPAN_FIELD: &str = "sandbox_id";

pub(super) fn record_ecw_sandbox_span_tag(sandbox_id: SandboxId) {
    let sandbox_id = sandbox_id.to_string();
    tracing::Span::current().record(ECW_SANDBOX_ID_SPAN_FIELD, sandbox_id.as_str());
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy)]
pub(super) struct EcwRequestLogContext {
    pub(super) sandbox_id: Option<SandboxId>,
}

impl EcwRequestLogContext {
    pub(super) fn sandbox(sandbox_id: SandboxId) -> Self {
        Self {
            sandbox_id: Some(sandbox_id),
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Default)]
pub(super) struct E2bRuntimeSpec {
    pub(super) template_id: Option<String>,
    pub(super) cpu_count: Option<u32>,
    pub(super) memory_mib: Option<u32>,
    pub(super) disk_size_mib: Option<u32>,
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/routes/public/network_policy/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;

#[test]
fn document_revision_etag_is_opaque_and_input_sensitive() {
    use pplx_space_net_common::NetworkPolicyV1;

    let deny_all = NetworkPolicyV1::deny_all();
    let allow_all = NetworkPolicyV1::allow_all();

    let base = document::revision_etag(3, false, &deny_all);
    assert_eq!(base, "\"e37a9ad8c5efe30ee649f0c0f2b02bd6\"");
    // Deterministic for identical inputs; quoted; no parseable structure.
    assert_eq!(base, document::revision_etag(3, false, &deny_all));
    assert!(base.starts_with('"') && base.ends_with('"'));
    assert!(!base.contains(':') && !base.contains("generation"));
    // Every input to the effective revision changes it: generation,
    // override-vs-inherited, and policy content.
    assert_ne!(base, document::revision_etag(4, false, &deny_all));
    assert_ne!(base, document::revision_etag(3, true, &deny_all));
    assert_ne!(base, document::revision_etag(3, false, &allow_all));
}

FILE: crates/libs/api/src/template_inventory_store/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn a_template_build_shape_needs_both_values() {
    assert_eq!(
        TemplateBuildShape::from_inventory(Some(4), Some(4096)),
        Some(TemplateBuildShape {
            vcpu: VcpuCount::try_from_u32(4).unwrap(),
            memory: MemorySize::mib(4096).unwrap(),
        })
    );
    assert_eq!(
        TemplateBuildShape::from_inventory(Some(0), Some(4096)),
        None
    );
    assert_eq!(TemplateBuildShape::from_inventory(Some(4), Some(-1)), None);
    assert_eq!(TemplateBuildShape::from_inventory(None, Some(4096)), None);
}

FILE: crates/libs/aws/client/src/query/tests.rs
================================================
use std::time::Duration;

use tokio::net::TcpListener;

use super::*;

#[tokio::test(flavor = "current_thread")]
async fn reqwest_transport_times_out_hung_response() {
    let listener = TcpListener::bind(("127.0.0.1", 0)).await.unwrap();
    let addr = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
    let server = tokio::spawn(async move {
        let (_socket, _) = listener.accept().await.unwrap();
        std::future::pending::<()>().await;
    });
    let transport =
        ReqwestTransport::with_timeouts(Duration::from_secs(1), Duration::from_millis(50));
    let request = Request::builder()
        .method("POST")
        .uri(format!("http://{addr}/"))
        .body(Vec::new())
        .unwrap();

    let err = transport.send(request).await.unwrap_err();
    server.abort();

    match err {
        AwsClientError::Http(err) => {
            assert!(err.is_timeout(), "expected reqwest timeout, got {err}");
        }
        err => panic!("expected reqwest timeout, got {err}"),
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/core/src/host/linux/gateway/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn identities_are_disjoint_reversible_and_reject_non_sandbox_peers() {
    let mut seen = std::collections::HashSet::new();
    for slot in [0, 1, 999, MAX_LINUX_NETWORK_SLOT] {
        let owner = LinuxUid::new(SANDBOX_UID_BASE + slot);
        let peer = gateway_uid(owner).unwrap();
        assert_ne!(owner, peer);
        assert!(seen.insert(owner));
        assert!(seen.insert(peer));
        assert_eq!(sandbox_uid(owner), Some(owner));
        assert_eq!(sandbox_uid(peer), Some(owner));
        assert_eq!(gateway_uid(peer), None);
    }
    for uid in [
        0,
        SANDBOX_UID_BASE - 1,
        SANDBOX_UID_BASE + MAX_LINUX_NETWORK_SLOT + 1,
        GATEWAY_UID_BASE - 1,
        GATEWAY_UID_BASE + MAX_LINUX_NETWORK_SLOT + 1,
        u32::MAX,
    ] {
        assert_eq!(sandbox_uid(LinuxUid::new(uid)), None);
    }
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn exact_boundary_succeeds_and_overflow_cannot_spend_remaining_capacity() {
    let mut bytes = InventoryBudget::new(2, 5);
    bytes.charge(3).unwrap();
    bytes.charge(3).unwrap_err();
    bytes.charge(2).unwrap();
    bytes.charge(0).unwrap_err();
    let mut items = InventoryBudget::new(1, usize::MAX);
    items.charge(0).unwrap();
    items.charge(0).unwrap_err();
    items.charge_bytes(usize::MAX).unwrap();
    items.charge_bytes(1).unwrap_err();
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/core/src/sandbox_storage/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn rootfs_entitlement_rejects_zero_and_over_limit_without_rounding() {
    for bytes in [1, 1024 * 1024, MAX_SANDBOX_ROOTFS_BYTES] {
        assert_eq!(validate_rootfs_bytes(bytes).unwrap().as_bytes(), bytes);
    }
    assert_eq!(validate_rootfs_bytes(0), Err(RootfsSizeError::Zero));
    for bytes in [MAX_SANDBOX_ROOTFS_BYTES + 1, u64::MAX] {
        assert!(matches!(
            validate_rootfs_bytes(bytes),
            Err(RootfsSizeError::TooLarge { .. })
        ));
    }
    assert_eq!(
        validate_rootfs_mib(128 * 1024).unwrap().as_bytes(),
        128 * 1024 * 1024 * 1024
    );
    validate_rootfs_mib(128 * 1024 + 1).unwrap_err();
    validate_rootfs_mib(u32::MAX).unwrap_err();
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/core/src/sql_locator/tests.rs

#[test]
fn literal_form_is_static_and_carries_module_and_line() {
    let expected_line = line!() + 1;
    let sql: &'static str = crate::sql!("SELECT 1");
    assert_eq!(
        sql,
        format!("/*pplx_space_common_core::sql_locator::tests:{expected_line}*/ SELECT 1")
    );
}

#[test]
fn locator_comment_is_a_single_whitespace_free_token() {
    let sql = crate::sql!("SELECT 1");
    let (comment, _) = sql.split_once("*/").expect("comment terminator");
    assert!(!comment.contains(char::is_whitespace));
}

#[test]
fn expr_form_prepends_locator_to_runtime_sql() {
    let table = "sandboxes";
    let expected_line = line!() + 1;
    let sql: String = crate::sql!(format!("DELETE FROM {table}"));
    assert_eq!(
        sql,
        format!(
            "/*pplx_space_common_core::sql_locator::tests:{expected_line}*/ \
                 DELETE FROM sandboxes"
        )
    );
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/core/src/types/sqlite.rs

//! SQLite encodes domain UUIDs as text to preserve existing node-local schemas.

use super::{JobId, NodeId, SandboxId};

macro_rules! sqlite_uuid {
    ($($name:ty),+ $(,)?) => {$(
        impl rusqlite::types::ToSql for $name {
            fn to_sql(&self) -> rusqlite::Result<rusqlite::types::ToSqlOutput<'_>> {
                Ok(self.to_string().into())
            }
        }

        impl rusqlite::types::FromSql for $name {
            fn column_result(value: rusqlite::types::ValueRef<'_>) -> rusqlite::types::FromSqlResult<Self> {
                value.as_str()?.parse().map_err(|error| rusqlite::types::FromSqlError::Other(Box::new(error)))
            }
        }
    )+};
}

sqlite_uuid!(JobId, NodeId, SandboxId);

mod tests;

FILE: crates/libs/common/grpc/src/server.rs

use tower::layer::util::{Identity, Stack};
use tower_http::metrics::InFlightRequestsLayer;

use crate::active_requests;

pub type GrpcServer = tonic::transport::Server<Stack<InFlightRequestsLayer, Identity>>;

/// The tonic server every SPACE gRPC listener starts from, wired for
/// active-request observation and nothing else.
///
/// Set no request timeout, keepalive, or connection age here: every listener
/// in the workspace is built from this, including the spaced bridges that
/// carry long-lived streams untimed. Shared timeout policy belongs in
/// `pplx_space_common_svc::grpc_server_builder`, which layers it on top.
pub fn grpc_server_builder() -> GrpcServer {
    tonic::transport::Server::builder()
        .max_frame_size(crate::BULK_HTTP2_MAX_FRAME_SIZE)
        .layer(active_requests::process_layer())
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn jittered_delay_stays_within_bounds() {
    let base = Duration::from_millis(100);
    for _ in 0..100 {
        let delay = jittered_delay(base, 0.2);
        assert!(delay >= Duration::from_millis(80));
        assert!(delay <= Duration::from_millis(120));
    }
}

#[test]
fn default_connect_policy_has_bounded_rollout_budget() {
    assert_eq!(
        DEFAULT_GRPC_CONNECT_RETRY_POLICY.max_elapsed,
        Duration::from_secs(10)
    );
    assert_eq!(
        DEFAULT_TCP_GRPC_CONNECT_RETRY_POLICY,
        DEFAULT_GRPC_CONNECT_RETRY_POLICY
    );
    assert!(DEFAULT_GRPC_CONNECT_RETRY_POLICY.max_delay <= Duration::from_secs(1));
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/io/src/lib.rs
================================================
//! Reusable I/O mechanics shared across SPACE services.
//!
//! This crate is the home for portable stream, compression, and
//! encryption building blocks that operate on bytes independently of
//! any product domain — Volume Manager remains the orchestrator and
//! authority that composes them into snapshot/artifact transfers, but
//! the mechanics themselves (and future btrfs full/diff stream
//! handling) live here so other consumers can reuse them without
//! depending on vol-mgr.
//!
//! The [`fs`] module provides streaming AES-256-GCM file/byte encryption,
//! while [`sparse`] provides a byte-oriented sparse-file writer. The crate
//! deliberately holds **no** BYOK/snapshot domain policy (which key, which
//! artifacts should be sparse, which AAD, where in S3): callers provide those
//! decisions and compose the shared mechanics.

pub mod fs;
#[cfg(all(unix, feature = "reflink"))]
pub mod reflink;
pub mod sparse;

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/http_listener.rs
================================================
//! TCP listeners for the services' HTTP servers.

use tokio::net::{TcpListener, TcpStream};

/// A listener whose accepted connections have Nagle's algorithm disabled.
pub type NodelayListener = axum::serve::TapIo<TcpListener, fn(&mut TcpStream)>;

/// Accept connections with `TCP_NODELAY` set.
///
/// `axum::serve` leaves Nagle enabled on a plain `TcpListener`. A response
/// written in two small pieces, headers and then a short body, then waits for
/// the client's delayed ACK before the body goes out, which adds about 40ms to
/// every small response on a reused connection.
pub fn nodelay(listener: TcpListener) -> NodelayListener {
    fn set_nodelay(tcp: &mut TcpStream) {
        if let Err(error) = tcp.set_nodelay(true) {
            tracing::debug!(%error, "failed to set TCP_NODELAY on an accepted connection");
        }
    }
    axum::serve::ListenerExt::tap_io(listener, set_nodelay as fn(&mut TcpStream))
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;

//! Reusable Redis client construction for SPACE services.

use ::redis::{RedisResult, cluster::ClusterClient};

/// Builds a lazy cluster-aware client from a Redis URL.
///
/// Construction validates configuration without connecting. The Redis crate
/// performs topology discovery and routing when a consumer requests a
/// connection.
pub fn cluster_client_from_url(url: &str) -> RedisResult<ClusterClient> {
    ClusterClient::new([url])
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/auth/template.rs

use tonic::{Request, Status};

use super::{NetworkPolicyMutationAuthority, NodeApiAuthenticatedCaller};

pub fn require_template_mutation_authority<T>(request: &Request<T>) -> Result<(), Status> {
    if request
        .extensions()
        .get::<NodeApiAuthenticatedCaller>()
        .is_some_and(|caller| {
            caller.network_policy_authority() == NetworkPolicyMutationAuthority::Granted
        })
    {
        Ok(())
    } else {
        Err(Status::permission_denied(
            "template mutation requires control-plane authority",
        ))
    }
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/dependency_drain/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;

#[test]
fn participant_rejects_symlink_lock_file() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let target = dir.path().join("host-file");
    let capability = dir.path().join("capability.lock");
    std::fs::write(&target, b"host state").unwrap();
    std::os::unix::fs::symlink(&target, &capability).unwrap();

    let result = DependencyDrainParticipant::register(
        &capability,
        dir.path().join("admission.lock"),
        dir.path().join("work.lock"),
    );

    assert!(
        matches!(result, Err(DependencyDrainError::Open { .. })),
        "dependency-drain locks must never follow a symlink: {result:?}"
    );
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/http_listener/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;
use axum::serve::Listener;

#[tokio::test]
async fn accepted_connections_have_nodelay_set() {
    let mut listener = nodelay(TcpListener::bind("127.0.0.1:0").await.unwrap());
    let address = listener.local_addr().unwrap();
    let client = TcpStream::connect(address).await.unwrap();
    let (accepted, peer) = listener.accept().await;
    assert_eq!(peer, client.local_addr().unwrap());
    assert!(accepted.nodelay().unwrap());
}

#[test]
fn advise_dontneed_accepts_regular_file() {
    let temp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = temp.path().join("rootfs.ext4");
    std::fs::write(&path, b"rootfs").unwrap();
    let file = std::fs::File::open(&path).unwrap();

    super::advise_dontneed(&file, &path, "test page cache");
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
#[test]
fn range_writeback_and_advice_accept_regular_file() {
    let temp = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    let path = temp.path().join("streamed-output");
    std::fs::write(&path, vec![0xa5; 64 * 1024]).unwrap();
    let file = std::fs::OpenOptions::new()
        .read(true)
        .write(true)
        .open(path)
        .unwrap();

    super::start_writeback_range(&file, 0, 64 * 1024).unwrap();
    super::wait_writeback_range(&file, 0, 64 * 1024).unwrap();
    super::advise_range_dontneed(&file, 0, 64 * 1024).unwrap();
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/peer_auth/os/mod.rs
================================================
//! Platform-gated peer process inspection.
//!
//! Mirrors the `host::linux` / `host::other` split in
//! `pplx-space-common-core`: every host has exactly one
//! `read_peer_process` implementation, selected at compile time.
//! The Linux path reads `/proc/<pid>/{status,exe}`; every other host
//! returns `io::ErrorKind::Unsupported` so opt-in process checks
//! fail closed.

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
mod linux;
#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
pub(super) use linux::{current_process_cap_effective, read_peer_process};

#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
mod other;
#[cfg(not(target_os = "linux"))]
pub(super) use other::read_peer_process;



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/peer_auth/os/other.rs
================================================
//! Non-Linux fallback for peer process inspection.
//!
//! `/proc` is Linux-only. On any other host this returns
//! `io::ErrorKind::Unsupported`, which the authorizer maps to
//! `UNAUTHENTICATED`. Opt-in process checks therefore **fail closed**
//! on non-Linux platforms; the rest of UDS peer auth (filesystem
//! perms + `SO_PEERCRED` uid match) is unaffected.

use super::super::PeerProcessSnapshot;

pub(in crate::peer_auth) fn read_peer_process(_pid: i32) -> std::io::Result<PeerProcessSnapshot> {
    Err(std::io::Error::new(
        std::io::ErrorKind::Unsupported,
        "peer process inspection requires Linux /proc",
    ))
}

            .is_none()

    ));

        .unwrap()

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/sql/node_registration.rs
================================================
//! Postgres registration reads for Kubernetes nodes.

use pplx_space_common_core::sql;
use pplx_space_common_core::types::{K8sNodeName, K8sNodeUid, NodeId};

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum NodeRegistrationError {
    #[error("node registration lookup failed: {0}")]
    Query(#[from] sqlx::Error),
}

/// Require both name and UID; a registration without a UID cannot match.
pub async fn registered_node(
    executor: impl sqlx::PgExecutor<'_>,
    name: &K8sNodeName,
    uid: &K8sNodeUid,
) -> Result<Option<NodeId>, NodeRegistrationError> {
    Ok(sqlx::query_scalar(sql!(
        "SELECT node_id FROM node_k8s
         WHERE k8s_node_name = $1 AND k8s_node_uid = $2 AND k8s_node_uid IS NOT NULL"
    ))
    .bind(name.as_str())
    .bind(uid.as_str())
    .fetch_optional(executor)
    .await?)
}

use super::managed_password;
use crate::core::types::DatabaseIdentity;
use crate::sql::{SPACE_DB_CREDS_PASSWORD_ENV, SPACE_DB_USER_PASSWORD_ENV};

#[test]
fn projected_passwords_pass_through() {
    for identity in [DatabaseIdentity::SpaceUser, DatabaseIdentity::SpaceCreds] {
        let password = managed_password(identity, Some("pw".to_owned())).unwrap();

        assert_eq!(password, "pw");
    }
}

#[test]
fn missing_password_error_names_the_identity_and_env_var() {
    for (identity, env_var) in [
        (DatabaseIdentity::SpaceUser, SPACE_DB_USER_PASSWORD_ENV),
        (DatabaseIdentity::SpaceCreds, SPACE_DB_CREDS_PASSWORD_ENV),
    ] {
        for absent in [None, Some(String::new())] {
            let error = managed_password(identity, absent).unwrap_err();
            let message = error.to_string();

            assert!(message.contains(env_var), "{message}");
            assert!(message.contains(identity.as_str()), "{message}");
        }
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/common/svc/src/types/grpc.rs

use tonic::Status;

use super::errors::DomainParseError;

/// Parse error type used by gRPC request converters.
///
/// This alias keeps service code explicit about where domain parse errors cross
/// a gRPC boundary.
///
/// ```
/// # use pplx_space_common_svc::types::GrpcParseError;
/// let err = GrpcParseError::new("invalid template");
/// assert_eq!(err.message(), "invalid template");
/// ```
pub type GrpcParseError = DomainParseError;

/// Converts a domain parse error into a gRPC `INVALID_ARGUMENT` status.
///
/// ```
/// # use pplx_space_common_svc::types::{invalid_argument, DomainParseError};
/// # use tonic::Code;
/// let status = invalid_argument(DomainParseError::new("invalid sandbox id"));
/// assert_eq!(status.code(), Code::InvalidArgument);
/// ```
pub fn invalid_argument(error: DomainParseError) -> Status {
    Status::invalid_argument(error.to_string())
}

fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let manifest_dir = std::env::var("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")?;
    let proto_root = std::path::Path::new(&manifest_dir).join("../../../../../proto");
    let file = proto_root.join("credential/manager/v1/network_credential.proto");

    let builder = tonic_prost_build::configure()
        .skip_debug([".space.credential.manager.v1.ResolvedNetworkCredential"])
        .type_attribute(
            ".space.credential.manager.v1.ResolvedNetworkCredential",
            "#[derive(zeroize::Zeroize, zeroize::ZeroizeOnDrop)]",
        );
    pplx_space_common_proto_build::compile_protos(builder, &[file], &[proto_root])?;
    Ok(())
}

FILE: crates/libs/credential/injection/core/src/validation.rs
================================================
//! Validation for reference-only credential locators.

use crate::secret_arn::SecretArn;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, thiserror::Error)]
#[error("credential reference is invalid: {0}")]
pub struct ReferenceValidationError(pub &'static str);

#[derive(Clone)]
pub enum ValidatedCredentialReference {
    AwsSecretsManager(SecretArn),
}

impl ValidatedCredentialReference {
    pub fn aws_secrets_manager(
        secret_arn: impl Into<String>,
    ) -> Result<Self, ReferenceValidationError> {
        SecretArn::parse(secret_arn)
            .map(Self::AwsSecretsManager)
            .map_err(|_| ReferenceValidationError("credential reference is malformed"))
    }
}

impl std::fmt::Debug for ValidatedCredentialReference {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut std::fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> std::fmt::Result {
        f.write_str("ValidatedCredentialReference(<redacted>)")
    }
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn secret_arn_requires_a_region_and_twelve_digit_account() {
    assert_eq!(
        parse_secret_arn("arn:aws:secretsmanager:us-west-2:123456789012:secret:smoke-AbCdEf")
            .unwrap(),
        ("123456789012", "us-west-2")
    );
    for arn in [
        "arn:aws:secretsmanager::123456789012:secret:smoke-AbCdEf",
        "arn:aws:secretsmanager:us-west-2::secret:smoke-AbCdEf",
        "arn:aws:secretsmanager:us-west-2:123:secret:smoke-AbCdEf",
    ] {
        assert!(
            matches!(parse_secret_arn(arn), Err(InvalidSecretArn)),
            "{arn}"
        );
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/db/migrator/build.rs
================================================
fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../db/postgres");
    watch("../../../../db/common");
    watch("../../../../db/sandbox-manager");
    watch("../../../../db/template-manager");
    watch("../../../../db/node-manager");
    watch("../../../../db/node-ctl");
    watch("../../../../db/volume-manager");
    watch("../../../../db/build-manager");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}

FILE: crates/libs/events/AGENTS.md
================================================
# `pplx-space-events` Agent Notes

Before changing this crate or adding sandbox event emission call sites, read
`README.md` in this directory.

Keep the sandbox event vocabulary domain-level. Add implementation identity in
`SandboxEventSource`, causality in `trigger`, and supporting context in
`SandboxEventDetails`; do not encode component names into `SandboxEventType`.

When adding, renaming, or removing an event type, source, or detail field:

- Update `README.md`.
- Update the tests in `src/lib.rs`.
- Keep the PR description's event table and coverage notes aligned with the
  emitted schema.

Do not add high-volume request telemetry here. This crate is for logical
sandbox state transitions, failed state-change requests, CAS/race outcomes,
cleanup paths, network allocation/policy/enforcement outcomes, port exposure
outcomes, and immutable snapshot/restore handoff facts.

FILE: crates/libs/events/src/phase.rs

use std::fmt;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum SandboxEventPhase {
    Requested,
    Started,
    Queued,
    Completed,
    Failed,
}

impl SandboxEventPhase {
    pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Requested => "requested",
            Self::Started => "started",
            Self::Queued => "queued",
            Self::Completed => "completed",
            Self::Failed => "failed",
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for SandboxEventPhase {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.as_str())
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/events/src/source.rs

use std::fmt;

#[derive(Debug, Clone, Copy, PartialEq, Eq, Hash)]
pub enum SandboxEventSource {
    Api,
    ClusterController,
    Substrate,
    SbxManager,
    NodeManager,
    NetGateway,
}

pub const SANDBOX_EVENT_SOURCES: &[SandboxEventSource] = &[
    SandboxEventSource::Api,
    SandboxEventSource::ClusterController,
    SandboxEventSource::Substrate,
    SandboxEventSource::SbxManager,
    SandboxEventSource::NodeManager,
    SandboxEventSource::NetGateway,
];

impl SandboxEventSource {
    pub const fn as_str(self) -> &'static str {
        match self {
            Self::Api => "api",
            Self::ClusterController => "cluster_controller",
            Self::Substrate => "substrate",
            Self::SbxManager => "sbx_manager",
            Self::NodeManager => "node_manager",
            Self::NetGateway => "net_gateway",
        }
    }
}

impl fmt::Display for SandboxEventSource {
    fn fmt(&self, f: &mut fmt::Formatter<'_>) -> fmt::Result {
        f.write_str(self.as_str())
    }
}

#[test]
fn unspecified_enum_values_are_rejected() {

            kind: proto::RuntimeKind::Unspecified as i32,
            version: "1.15.1".to_string(),

}

FILE: crates/libs/internal/api/server/src/api_key_store.rs
================================================
//! API-key authorization for the ECW data-plane resolver.

use pplx_space_common_core::{sql, types::OrgId};

pub(super) async fn authorizes_ecw_sandbox(
    executor: impl sqlx::PgExecutor<'_>,
    key_hash: &str,
    org_id: OrgId,
) -> Result<bool, sqlx::Error> {
    sqlx::query_scalar(sql!(
        "SELECT EXISTS (
             SELECT 1 FROM api_keys
             WHERE org_id = $1 AND key_hash = $2
               AND 'ecw' = ANY(allowed_surfaces)
         )"
    ))
    .bind(org_id)
    .bind(key_hash)
    .fetch_one(executor)
    .await
}

        1,
        &[tag("method", method), tag("mode", mode)],

}

}

//! Process-level health of the shared Tokio runtime.

use std::time::Duration;

use crate::emit::gauge;

#[derive(Clone, Copy, Debug, PartialEq)]
pub struct RuntimeHealth {
    pub num_workers: usize,
    pub num_alive_tasks: usize,
    pub global_queue_depth: usize,
    pub busy_ratio: f64,
    pub blocked_workers: usize,
}

pub fn health(sample: RuntimeHealth) {
    gauge("tokio_runtime.num_workers", sample.num_workers as f64, &[]);
    gauge(
        "tokio_runtime.num_alive_tasks",
        sample.num_alive_tasks as f64,
        &[],
    );
    gauge(
        "tokio_runtime.global_queue_depth",
        sample.global_queue_depth as f64,
        &[],
    );
    gauge("tokio_runtime.busy_ratio", sample.busy_ratio, &[]);
    gauge(
        "tokio_runtime.blocked_workers",
        sample.blocked_workers as f64,
        &[],
    );
}

pub fn scheduling_delay(delay: Duration) {
    gauge(
        "tokio_runtime.scheduling_delay_ms",
        delay.as_secs_f64() * 1_000.0,
        &[],
    );
}

FILE: crates/libs/metrics/src/gateway/tests.rs

use super::with_reserved_dns_hickory_metrics;
use crate::emit::test_capture::capture_counters_guard;

#[test]
fn absolute_updates_are_adapted_to_increments_without_double_counting() {
    let capture = capture_counters_guard();
    with_reserved_dns_hickory_metrics(|| {
        let counter = metrics::counter!("hickory_resolver_cache_hit_total");
        counter.increment(3);
        counter.absolute(10);
        counter.absolute(8);
        counter.absolute(13);
    });
    let captured = capture.finish();

    assert_eq!(
        captured
            .iter()
            .map(|counter| counter.value)
            .collect::<Vec<_>>(),
        vec![3, 7, 3]
    );
    assert!(captured.iter().all(|counter| {
        counter.name == "gateway.reserved_dns.dns_cache" && counter.tags == ["outcome:hit"]
    }));
}

fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {

    let proto_root = std::path::Path::new(&manifest_dir).join("../../../../proto");

            "#[derive(Eq, Hash)]",

    pplx_space_common_proto_build::compile_protos(builder, &[policy_v1_file], &[proto_root])?;

    Ok(())
}

FILE: crates/libs/net/common/src/orig_dst/linux/tests.rs

use super::*;
use std::net::Ipv4Addr;

#[test]
fn converts_original_destination_sockaddr() {
    let sock = SockaddrIn::from(SocketAddrV4::new(Ipv4Addr::new(203, 0, 113, 7), 5353));
    let parsed = sockaddr_in_to_v4(sock.into()).expect("valid sockaddr");

    assert_eq!(parsed.ip(), &Ipv4Addr::new(203, 0, 113, 7));
    assert_eq!(parsed.port(), 5353);
}

#[test]
fn rejects_non_ipv4_sockaddr() {
    let mut raw: libc::sockaddr_in =
        SockaddrIn::from(SocketAddrV4::new(Ipv4Addr::new(203, 0, 113, 7), 5353)).into();
    raw.sin_family = libc::AF_UNIX as libc::sa_family_t;

    assert!(sockaddr_in_to_v4(raw).is_none());
}

FILE: crates/libs/net/gateway/server/src/inherited.rs
================================================
use std::io;
use std::os::fd::{FromRawFd, RawFd};

use tokio::net::TcpListener;

pub fn tcp_listener_from_fd(fd: RawFd) -> io::Result<TcpListener> {
    // SAFETY: node-manager passes the gateway a valid, owned TCP listener fd.
    // `from_raw_fd` takes ownership so the fd is closed exactly once.
    let listener = unsafe { std::net::TcpListener::from_raw_fd(fd) };
    listener.set_nonblocking(true)?;
    TcpListener::from_std(listener)
}

pub fn udp_socket_from_fd(fd: RawFd) -> io::Result<std::net::UdpSocket> {
    // SAFETY: node-manager passes the gateway a valid, owned UDP socket fd.
    // `from_raw_fd` takes ownership so the fd is closed exactly once.
    let socket = unsafe { std::net::UdpSocket::from_raw_fd(fd) };
    socket.set_nonblocking(true)?;
    Ok(socket)
}

FILE: crates/libs/net/gateway/server/src/known_egress/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn reports_only_configured_dns_and_tcp_hosts() {
    let state = KnownEgressState::new(vec!["egress-proxy.example.test".parse().unwrap()]);
    let dns = "127.0.0.1:53".parse().unwrap();
    let tcp = "127.0.0.1:443".parse().unwrap();
    let mut query = vec![
        0x12, 0x34, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x01, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00, 0x00,
    ];
    for label in "egress-proxy.example.test".split('.') {
        query.push(u8::try_from(label.len()).unwrap());
        query.extend_from_slice(label.as_bytes());
    }
    query.extend_from_slice(&[0, 0, 1, 0, 1]);

    assert!(state.report_dns_failure("test", dns, &query, "denied"));
    assert!(state.report_tcp_failure(
        "test",
        tcp,
        &"EGRESS-PROXY.EXAMPLE.TEST.".parse().unwrap(),
        "connect"
    ));
    assert!(!state.report_tcp_failure("test", tcp, &"other.example".parse().unwrap(), "connect"));
}

use super::*;

#[tokio::test]
async fn unconfigured_activation_preserves_startup_and_refuses_only_delegated_wake() {
    if !pplx_space_test_env::enter_test_process(&[("SPACE_INGRESS_ACTIVATION_ENDPOINT", None)]) {
        return;
    }
    let client = RemoteIngressActivation::from_env()
        .await
        .expect("route resolver must start without a new listener configuration");
    let error = client
        .prepare(SandboxId::new(), Duration::from_secs(1))
        .await
        .unwrap_err();
    assert_eq!(error.code(), tonic::Code::Unavailable);
    assert_eq!(
        error.message(),
        "ingress activation endpoint is not configured"
    );
}

FILE: crates/libs/node/ctl/server/build.rs
================================================
fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../../db/node-ctl");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/node/ctl/server/src/util.rs
================================================
pub fn dedupe_non_empty(values: Vec<String>) -> Vec<String> {
    let mut out = Vec::with_capacity(values.len());
    for value in values {
        let value = value.trim();
        if value.is_empty() {
            continue;
        }
        let value = value.to_string();
        if !out.contains(&value) {
            out.push(value);
        }
    }
    out
}

FILE: crates/libs/node/mgr/server/build.rs
================================================
fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../../db/node-manager");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}

FILE: crates/libs/node/mgr/server/src/clone_cleanup/tests.rs

use super::*;

#[test]
fn disposal_budget_bounds_work_and_a_retry_finishes_partial_cleanup() {
    let directory = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    for name in ["first", "second", "third"] {
        std::fs::write(directory.path().join(name), b"partial clone").unwrap();
    }
    let root = File::open(directory.path()).unwrap();
    let mut budget = Budget {
        remaining: 1,
        deadline: Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(60),
    };
    assert!(matches!(
        remove_contents(&root, &mut budget, 0),
        Err(CloneDisposalError::BudgetExhausted)
    ));
    assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(directory.path()).unwrap().count(), 2);
    let mut retry_budget = Budget {
        remaining: 3,
        deadline: Instant::now() + Duration::from_secs(60),
    };
    remove_contents(&root, &mut retry_budget, 0).unwrap();
    assert_eq!(std::fs::read_dir(directory.path()).unwrap().count(), 0);
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn only_one_resolved_block_device_is_ownership_evidence() {
    let root = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    single_device(root.path()).unwrap_err();
    let device = root.path().join("dm-5");
    std::fs::create_dir(&device).unwrap();
    single_device(root.path()).unwrap_err();
    for (raw, expected) in [
        ("253:5\n", Some((253, 5))),
        ("0:42\n", None),
        ("garbage", None),
        ("253:", None),
    ] {
        std::fs::write(device.join("dev"), raw).unwrap();
        assert_eq!(single_device(root.path()).ok(), expected, "{raw}");
    }
    std::fs::write(device.join("dev"), "253:5\n").unwrap();
    std::fs::create_dir(root.path().join("dm-6")).unwrap();
    single_device(root.path()).unwrap_err();
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn default_route_probe_distinguishes_absent_space_link_from_failed_dump() {
    assert_eq!(
        interpret_default_route_output(
            true,
            b"default via 192.168.64.1 dev en0 proto dhcp\n",
            b"",
        )
        .unwrap(),
        false
    );
    assert_eq!(
        interpret_default_route_output(
            true,
            b"default via 10.12.0.1 dev sveth-000001 proto static\n",
            b"",
        )
        .unwrap(),
        true
    );
    assert_eq!(
        interpret_default_route_output(true, b"default via 10.12.0.1 dev vpeer-000001\n", b"",)
            .unwrap(),
        true
    );
    assert_eq!(
        interpret_default_route_output(true, b"default via 10.12.0.1 metric 100\n", b"",)
            .unwrap(),
        false
    );
    assert!(interpret_default_route_output(false, b"", b"ip route failed").is_err());
}

FILE: crates/libs/node/mgr/server/src/node_state_sync/tests.rs
================================================
use std::sync::atomic::{AtomicUsize, Ordering};

/// Counts node-status marks in the process instead of notifying node-ctl.
#[derive(Debug, Default)]
pub(crate) struct RecordingNodeStatusDirtySink {
    marks: AtomicUsize,
}

impl RecordingNodeStatusDirtySink {
    /// Marks recorded since the last call.
    pub(crate) fn take(&self) -> usize {
        self.marks.swap(0, Ordering::AcqRel)
    }
}

impl super::NodeStatusDirtySink for RecordingNodeStatusDirtySink {
    fn mark_node_status_dirty(&self) {
        self.marks.fetch_add(1, Ordering::AcqRel);
    }
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn directory_census_never_returns_a_successful_truncation() {
    let dir = tempfile::tempdir().unwrap();
    std::fs::create_dir(dir.path().join("one")).unwrap();
    std::fs::create_dir(dir.path().join("two")).unwrap();
    std::fs::write(dir.path().join("ignored-file"), []).unwrap();
    for (remaining, age) in [(1, Duration::ZERO), (100, Duration::from_secs(3))] {
        DirectoryBudget {
            remaining,
            started: Instant::now() - age,
        }
        .directories(dir.path())
        .unwrap_err();
    }
    assert_eq!(
        DirectoryBudget::default()
            .directories(dir.path())
            .unwrap()
            .len(),
        2
    );
    DirectoryBudget::default()
        .directories(&dir.path().join("ignored-file"))
        .unwrap_err();
    assert!(
        DirectoryBudget::default()
            .directories(&dir.path().join("absent"))
            .unwrap()
            .is_empty()
    );
}

//! Where sandbox cgroups live, for readers that do not manage them.

use std::path::PathBuf;

pub(crate) const CGROUP_BASE: &str = "/sys/fs/cgroup/space";

/// The cgroup holding a sandbox's Firecracker process and vCPU threads, where
/// its CPU, memory and pids limits and usage live; `sbx-{id}` holds only the
/// sidecars.
pub fn jailer_cgroup_path(sandbox_id: &str) -> PathBuf {
    PathBuf::from(format!("{CGROUP_BASE}/{sandbox_id}"))
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn adoption_requires_all_uid_and_gid_credentials() {
    for (status, expected) in [
        (
            "Uid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\nGid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\n",
            true,
        ),
        (
            "Uid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\nGid:\t2000000 2000000 2000000 2000000\n",
            false,
        ),
        (
            "Uid:\t10000 10000 0 10000\nGid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\n",
            false,
        ),
        ("Uid:\t10000 10000\nGid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\n", false),
        ("Uid:\t10000 10000 10000 10000\n", false),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(credentials_match(status, 10000, 10000), expected);
    }
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn live_runtime_pid_removes_stale_symlink() -> Result<(), Box<dyn std::error::Error>> {
    let sandbox_id = SandboxId::new();
    let tempdir = tempfile::tempdir()?;
    let pid_link = tempdir.path().join("fc.pid");
    unix_fs::symlink("/proc/999999999", &pid_link)?;

    let pid_link = pid_link.to_string_lossy();
    assert_eq!(live_runtime_pid_at(&sandbox_id, &pid_link)?, None);
    assert!(!std::path::Path::new(pid_link.as_ref()).exists());

    Ok(())
}

//! Log-facing views of the shared process identity.

use crate::identity::Identity;

pub const SPACE_SERVICE_NAME: &str = "SPACE_SERVICE_NAME";
pub const SPACE_ENV: &str = "SPACE_ENV";
pub const SPACE_VERSION: &str = "SPACE_VERSION";

/// Resolve log service identity while accepting legacy Datadog deployments.
pub fn service_name(default: &str) -> String {
    Identity::from_env(default).log_service_name().to_string()
}

/// Resolve the deployment environment used by log context and disclosure gates.
/// A neutral setting takes precedence over the legacy Datadog setting.
pub fn environment() -> Option<String> {
    Identity::from_env("").log_environment().map(str::to_string)
}

pub(crate) fn version() -> Option<String> {
    Identity::from_env("").log_version().map(str::to_string)
}

FILE: crates/libs/placement/machine/src/model/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;

fn generation(value: i64) -> ExecutionGeneration {
    ExecutionGeneration::new(value).expect("a positive generation is valid")
}

#[test]
fn for_generation_re_pairs_the_generation_and_keeps_lease_and_sandbox() {
    let sandbox = SandboxId::new();
    let lease = LeaseToken::mint();
    let fence = PlacementFence {
        sandbox,
        generation: generation(1),
        lease,
    };

    let re_paired = fence.for_generation(generation(2));

    assert_eq!(
        re_paired.generation(),
        generation(2),
        "the observed generation replaces the fence's"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        (re_paired.sandbox(), re_paired.lease()),
        (sandbox, lease),
        "ownership stays the lease: sandbox and token are untouched"
    );
}

#[test]
fn claim_kind_strings_are_stable() {

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]
#[cfg_attr(
    feature = "serde",
    serde(tag = "provider", rename_all = "snake_case", deny_unknown_fields)
)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "schema", derive(schemars::JsonSchema))]
pub enum RegisterCredentialReferenceRequest {
    AwsSecretsManager {
        request_id: String,
        secret_arn: String,
    },
}

impl RegisterCredentialReferenceRequest {
    #[must_use]
    pub fn request_id(&self) -> &str {
        match self {
            Self::AwsSecretsManager { request_id, .. } => request_id,
        }
    }
}

#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq, Eq)]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", derive(serde::Serialize, serde::Deserialize))]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "serde", serde(deny_unknown_fields))]
#[cfg_attr(feature = "schema", derive(schemars::JsonSchema))]
pub struct CredentialReference {
    pub credential_reference_id: String,
}

FILE: crates/libs/sbx/mgr/migrations/build.rs
================================================
fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../../db/sandbox-manager");
    watch("../../../../../db/common");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}

//! Observational liveness from node-manager's runtime PID symlinks.

use std::path::Path;

use pplx_space_common_core::host::linux::paths::SANDBOX_RUN_DIR;

pub(crate) fn sidecar_alive(sandbox_id: &str, service_name: &str) -> bool {
    sidecar_live_pid(sandbox_id, service_name).is_some()
}

pub(crate) fn sidecar_live_pid(sandbox_id: &str, service_name: &str) -> Option<u32> {
    let path = format!("{SANDBOX_RUN_DIR}/{sandbox_id}/{service_name}.pid");
    live_pid_from_symlink(Path::new(&path))
}

fn live_pid_from_symlink(path: &Path) -> Option<u32> {
    // A regular file or dangling link is not a running sidecar. This is only
    // observational evidence; callers needing ownership still compare the PID
    // against the committed execution identity.
    if !path.exists() {
        return None;
    }
    std::fs::read_link(path)
        .ok()?
        .file_name()?
        .to_str()?
        .parse()
        .ok()
        .filter(|pid| *pid != 0)
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn gate_semantics_cover_both_extremes() {
    assert!(gate_engaged(0.0, 0.0), "engage 0 = always-on");
    assert!(gate_engaged(0.41, 0.40));
    assert!(!gate_engaged(0.39, 0.40));
    assert!(!gate_engaged(0.99, 1.0), "engage 1.0 = residue-only");
}

#[test]
fn misconfigured_pair_derives_target_below_engage() {
    let (engage, target) = validate_fraction_pair("test", 0.40, 0.40, "E", "T");
    assert!((engage - 0.40).abs() < 1e-9);
    assert!((target - 0.30).abs() < 1e-9);
    // Clamping out-of-range inputs.
    let (engage, target) = validate_fraction_pair("test", 7.0, -1.0, "E", "T");
    assert!((engage - 1.0).abs() < 1e-9);
    assert!(target.abs() < 1e-9);
    // engage 0 (always-on) never warns or derives.
    let (engage, target) = validate_fraction_pair("test", 0.0, 0.0, "E", "T");
    assert!(engage.abs() < 1e-9);
    assert!(target.abs() < 1e-9);
}

use super::*;

#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]
async fn node_manager_state_read_respects_its_deadline() {
    let read = std::future::pending::<Result<FirecrackerVmState, FirecrackerStateReadError>>();

    let error = bounded_firecracker_vm_state_read(read, Duration::from_millis(10))
        .await
        .expect_err("a wedged node-manager read must time out");

    assert!(matches!(
        error,
        FirecrackerStateReadError::Timeout { timeout_ms: 10 }
    ));
}

#[test]
fn state_read_errors_preserve_rpc_code_without_peer_payloads() {
    for code in [tonic::Code::Unimplemented, tonic::Code::Unavailable] {
        let error =
            FirecrackerStateReadError::from(tonic::Status::new(code, "untrusted-peer-body"));
        assert!(!error.to_string().contains("untrusted-peer-body"));
        assert!(matches!(error, FirecrackerStateReadError::Rpc(status) if status.code() == code));
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/sbx/mgr/server/src/service/execution_startup.rs
================================================
use super::*;

impl SandboxManagerService {
    /// Validate local execution ownership under the process mutex and recovery
    /// work gate. Dependency availability is handled before recovery admission.
    pub(crate) async fn prepare_execution_authority(&self) -> Result<(), Status> {
        let db = self.db.clone();
        tokio::task::spawn_blocking(move || {
            let db = db
                .lock()
                .map_err(|_| Status::unavailable("sandbox database mutex poisoned"))?;
            let unadopted = crate::db::unbound_node_executions(&db).map_err(|error| {
                Status::unavailable(format!("read execution preflight: {error}"))
            })?;
            pplx_space_node_mgr_client::execution_fence::execution_preflight_result(&unadopted)
        })
        .await
        .map_err(|error| Status::unavailable(format!("execution preflight task: {error}")))?
    }
}

FILE: crates/libs/sbx/mgr/server/src/service/snapshot_capture.rs

//! Environment-bound capture uses an identity old nodes cannot execute.

use pplx_space_common_core::types::ExecutionMetadata;
use pplx_space_node_mgr_client::proto::{CreateSnapshotRequest, EnvironmentSnapshotInput};

pub(crate) fn bind(
    mut request: CreateSnapshotRequest,
    execution: Option<&ExecutionMetadata>,
) -> CreateSnapshotRequest {
    if let Some(execution) = execution
        && super::validation::is_non_default_execution(execution)
        && let Some(reference) = execution.environment()
    {
        request.environment = Some(EnvironmentSnapshotInput {
            sandbox_id: std::mem::take(&mut request.sandbox_id),
            reference: reference.to_string(),
        });
    }
    request
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn cpu_stat_usage_usec_parses_the_usage_line() {
    let content = "usage_usec 41398047\nuser_usec 40000000\nsystem_usec 1398047\n";
    assert_eq!(cpu_stat_usage_usec(content), Some(41_398_047));
    assert_eq!(cpu_stat_usage_usec("user_usec 1\n"), None);
}

#[test]
fn inspect_warnings_are_rate_limited_per_sandbox() {
    let warnings = PerSandboxWarnings::new(Duration::from_secs(60));
    let start = Instant::now();
    assert!(warnings.admit("a", start));
    assert!(!warnings.admit("a", start + Duration::from_secs(59)));
    // Another sandbox is not held back by the first one's warning.
    assert!(warnings.admit("b", start + Duration::from_secs(1)));
    assert!(warnings.admit("a", start + Duration::from_secs(60)));
    // Admitting a warning drops every expired entry.
    assert!(warnings.admit("c", start + Duration::from_secs(120)));
    assert_eq!(warnings.last_warned.lock().unwrap().len(), 1);
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn completion_is_published_with_zero_count_and_old_execution_is_isolated() {
    let old = Protection::default();
    let replacement = Protection::default();
    let first = old.acquire();
    let second = old.acquire();
    let current = replacement.acquire();
    drop(first);
    assert_eq!(old.observation().0, 1);
    assert_eq!(old.observation().1, None);
    drop(second);
    let (count, release, age) = old.observation();
    assert_eq!(count, 0);
    assert!(release.is_some());
    assert!(age.is_none());
    assert_eq!(replacement.observation().0, 1);
    // Failed persistence leaves the observation available for another scan.
    assert_eq!(old.observation().1, release);
    old.consumed(release.unwrap());
    assert_eq!(old.observation().1, None);
    drop(current);
}

use super::*;
use prost::Message;
use std::collections::BTreeSet;

fn served_spaced_rpcs() -> BTreeSet<String> {
    let descriptor = prost_types::FileDescriptorSet::decode(
        pplx_space_sbx_mgr_client::proto::FILE_DESCRIPTOR_SET,
    )
    .expect("sandbox-manager descriptor decodes");

    descriptor
        .file
        .into_iter()
        .flat_map(|file| file.service)
        .filter(|service| service.name.as_deref() == Some("SandboxManager"))
        .flat_map(|service| service.method)
        .filter_map(|method| method.name)
        .filter(|name| name.starts_with("Spaced"))
        .collect()
}

#[test]
fn every_served_spaced_rpc_has_exactly_one_policy() {
    let served = served_spaced_rpcs();
    let classified = SpacedRpc::ALL
        .iter()
        .map(|rpc| rpc.as_str().to_string())
        .collect::<BTreeSet<_>>();

    assert_eq!(classified, served);
    assert_eq!(
        SpacedRpc::ALL.len(),
        classified.len(),
        "duplicate bridge policy entry"
    );
}

    command -v "$program" >/dev/null 2>&1 || exit 127

while :; do
    timeout 2s systemctl show --no-pager -p Id -p LoadState -p ActiveState -p SubState -p InactiveExitTimestampMonotonic -- "$@" 2>/dev/null || true
    printf '\n.\n'
    sleep 0.3 || exit 127
done

FILE: crates/libs/snapshot/storage/README.md
================================================
# Snapshot byte storage

`pplx-space-snapshot-storage` owns reusable operations over durable snapshot
bytes. It is separate from `pplx-space-snapshot-store`, which owns the
PostgreSQL catalog.

The current operation deletes every object under one parsed snapshot prefix.
An empty prefix is idempotent success. Backend implementations must surface
partial deletion as failure so callers never finalize catalog metadata while
bytes remain.

Vendor integrations are optional features. The S3 implementation is behind
the `s3` feature so catalog-only and substrate-neutral crates do not inherit
the AWS SDK.

FILE: crates/libs/snapshot/storage/src/lib.rs

#![doc = include_str!("../README.md")]

use pplx_space_common_core::types::StorageUri;

#[cfg(feature = "s3")]
pub mod s3;

/// Outcome of deleting one snapshot prefix.
#[derive(Debug, Clone, PartialEq, Eq)]
pub enum DeletePrefixOutcome {
    Deleted { count: u64 },
    AlreadyGone,
}

#[derive(Debug, thiserror::Error)]
pub enum DeletePrefixError {
    #[error("object store delete failed: {0}")]
    Backend(String),
}

/// Backend-neutral operation for deleting the bytes below a parsed snapshot
/// prefix. Partial backend deletion must return an error.
pub trait StorageBackend: Send + Sync + 'static {
    fn delete_prefix(
        &self,
        uri: &StorageUri,
    ) -> impl std::future::Future<Output = Result<DeletePrefixOutcome, DeletePrefixError>> + Send;
}

FILE: crates/libs/spaced/common/src/admission_queue.rs
================================================
//! The bounded wait spaced-host and the guest give a request that finds every
//! slot of its budget busy, so a short burst queues instead of failing.
//!
//! Queued requests are not decoded, so the slot budgets stay the
//! decode-memory bound. The waiter count matches node-api's default
//! per-sandbox concurrency limit; the wait drains a full queue at the slot
//! hold times of a CPU-starved guest while staying well inside the host's
//! stream-open budget. A file operation can wait at both hops, so at most
//! twice [`MAX_WAIT`] before its work starts.

use std::time::Duration;

pub const WAITERS: usize = 64;
pub const MAX_WAIT: Duration = Duration::from_secs(2);

FILE: crates/libs/spaced/common/src/filesystem_limits.rs
================================================
//! Per-daemon payload budgets, independent of worker and retry-record counts.
// Eight requests leave room for decoder/transport buffers alongside the 4 MiB
// public message cap. Admission is held through execution cleanup, not just
// through the caller's lifetime. Metadata/watch/process RPCs use other budgets.
pub const PAYLOAD_SLOTS: usize = 8;
// Repeated protobuf entries amplify decoded heap beyond wire bytes. Charge a
// batch four slots (at most two batches), including during decoding and cleanup.
pub const BATCH_REQUEST_WEIGHT: u32 = 4;
pub const PAYLOAD_METHODS: &[(&str, u32)] = &[
    ("Write", 1),
    ("WriteBatch", BATCH_REQUEST_WEIGHT),
    ("Rename", 1),
];
pub const MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES: usize = pplx_space_common_core::spaced::MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES;
pub const WRITE_CHUNK_BYTES: usize = 64 * 1024;
pub const WRITE_BUFFER_FRAMES: usize = 2;



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/spaced/common/src/process_limits.rs
================================================
//! Predecode budgets for process request payloads, separate from live processes.
//! Start's repeated argv/environment fields amplify decoded heap; input can wait
//! on a blocked stdin writer. Overload rejects without decoding or applying work.
pub const PAYLOAD_SLOTS: usize = 8;
pub const START_PAYLOAD_SLOTS: u32 = 4;
pub const PAYLOAD_METHODS: &[(&str, u32)] = &[("Start", START_PAYLOAD_SLOTS), ("SendInput", 1)];
pub const MAX_MESSAGE_BYTES: usize = 4 * 1024 * 1024;

FILE: crates/libs/spaced/guest/server/src/filesystem/tests.rs
================================================
use super::*;
use std::time::Duration;

// A burst larger than the slot budget waits for a slot instead of failing at
// once; the queue's bounds are `QueuedSlots`'.
#[tokio::test(start_paused = true)]
async fn a_file_operation_burst_queues_until_a_slot_frees() {
    let service = FilesystemWorkerService::new(
        GuestUserName::parse("user".to_string()).expect("user name"),
        CancellationToken::new(),
    );
    let held = service
        .operation_admission
        .acquire(MAX_CONCURRENT_FILE_OPERATIONS as u32)
        .await
        .unwrap();
    let waiter = tokio::spawn({
        let service = service.clone();
        async move { service.acquire_file_operation_slot().await }
    });
    tokio::time::sleep(Duration::from_millis(500)).await;
    assert!(!waiter.is_finished());
    drop(held);
    let _admitted = waiter
        .await
        .unwrap()
        .expect("a queued operation is admitted once a slot frees");
}

FILE: crates/libs/spaced/guest/server/src/filesystem/watch/event/tests.rs

use pplx_space_spaced_common::proto::bridge_guest::FileEventKind;

use super::*;

#[test]
fn watch_event_converts_to_proto() {
    let event = WatchEvent::new(
        PathBuf::from("/tmp/file.txt"),
        PathBuf::from("file.txt"),
        FileEventKind::Modified,
        42,
    )
    .with_is_regular_file(Some(true));
    let proto = event.to_proto();

    assert_eq!(proto.path, "file.txt");
    assert_eq!(event.absolute_path(), Path::new("/tmp/file.txt"));
    assert_eq!(proto.kind(), FileEventKind::Modified);
    assert_eq!(proto.observed_at, Some(unix_ms_to_timestamp(42)));
    assert_eq!(event.is_regular_file(), Some(true));
}

use super::*;

#[test]
fn relative_paths_resolve_under_account_home() {
    let home = Path::new("/guest/home");
    assert_eq!(
        normalize_for_home("", home).unwrap().expose(),
        "/guest/home"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        normalize_for_home("src/../main.py", home).unwrap().expose(),
        "/guest/home/main.py"
    );
    assert_eq!(
        normalize_for_home("../../etc/hosts", home)
            .unwrap()
            .expose(),
        "/etc/hosts"
    );
}

#[test]
fn absolute_paths_stay_rooted_at_guest_root() {
    let home = Path::new("/guest/home");
    assert_eq!(
        normalize_for_home("/tmp/file", home).unwrap().expose(),
        "/tmp/file"
    );
}

#[test]
fn non_linux_paths_are_rejected() {
    let home = Path::new("/guest/home");
    for path in ["C:\\Users\\user", "//server/share", "bad\npath"] {
        assert_eq!(
            normalize_for_home(path, home).unwrap_err().code(),
            tonic::Code::InvalidArgument
        );
    }
}

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
mod linux;
#[cfg(not(unix))]
mod other;
#[cfg(all(unix, not(target_os = "linux")))]
mod unix;

#[cfg(target_os = "linux")]
pub(super) use linux::{GuestAccount, ResolveGuestAccountError};
#[cfg(not(unix))]
pub(super) use other::{GuestAccount, ResolveGuestAccountError};
#[cfg(all(unix, not(target_os = "linux")))]
pub(super) use unix::{GuestAccount, ResolveGuestAccountError};

#[cfg(any(target_os = "linux", test))]
fn supplementary_groups_match(
    mut current: Vec<nix::unistd::Gid>,
    requested: &[nix::unistd::Gid],
    primary: nix::unistd::Gid,
) -> bool {
    // getgrouplist includes the primary group; getgroups need not include it.
    current.push(primary);
    current.sort_unstable_by_key(|gid| gid.as_raw());
    current.dedup();
    let mut requested = requested.to_vec();
    requested.push(primary);
    requested.sort_unstable_by_key(|gid| gid.as_raw());
    requested.dedup();
    current == requested
}

#[cfg(test)]
mod tests;



================================================
FILE: crates/libs/spaced/guest/server/src/identity/tests.rs
================================================
use super::supplementary_groups_match;
use nix::unistd::Gid;

#[test]
fn same_user_requires_equivalent_supplementary_groups() {
    for (current, requested, matches) in [
        (vec![], vec![1000], true),
        (vec![10, 20], vec![20, 1000, 10, 10], true),
        (vec![10, 20], vec![1000, 10], false),
        (vec![10], vec![1000, 10, 20], false),
    ] {
        assert_eq!(
            supplementary_groups_match(
                current.into_iter().map(Gid::from_raw).collect(),
                &requested.into_iter().map(Gid::from_raw).collect::<Vec<_>>(),
                Gid::from_raw(1000),
            ),
            matches
        );
    }
}

//! Single integration-test binary for `pplx-space-spaced-guest-server`.
//!
//! `filesystem`, `meta`, and `process` were previously three standalone
//! `tests/*.rs` binaries, each linking the full dependency graph
//! independently. Folding them into one binary with submodules cuts that
//! link cost to once per `cargo test` invocation. `support` holds the shared
//! fixtures they all previously included per-binary via `#[path]`; it is
//! declared once here instead of once per consumer.

#![cfg(test)]

#[path = "support/guest_meta.rs"]

#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "support/guest_service.rs"]
mod guest_service;
#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "support/process_events.rs"]
mod process_events;

#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "it/filesystem.rs"]
mod filesystem;
#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "it/meta.rs"]
mod meta;
#[cfg(unix)]
#[path = "it/process.rs"]
mod process;

//! Real Linux worker launch, credential switch and periodic OTLP export.
//! Ignored by default: the real credential switch requires root. Run on Linux:
//! ```sh

//!   --config 'target.x86_64-unknown-linux-gnu.runner=["sudo","-n"]' -- --ignored

//! ignored tests, so missing privileges there fail instead of skipping coverage.

use super::*;

#[test]
fn wait_timeout_allows_zero_for_try_now() {
    let wait_timeout = validate_optional_wait_timeout(Some(&prost_types::Duration {
        seconds: 0,
        nanos: 0,
    }))
    .unwrap();

    assert_eq!(wait_timeout, Some(std::time::Duration::ZERO));
}

#[test]
fn wait_timeout_rejects_negative_values() {
    let error = validate_optional_wait_timeout(Some(&prost_types::Duration {
        seconds: -1,
        nanos: 0,
    }))
    .unwrap_err();

    assert_eq!(error.code(), tonic::Code::InvalidArgument);
}

    prost_types::Timestamp {
        seconds: (milliseconds / 1_000) as i64,
        nanos: ((milliseconds % 1_000) * 1_000_000) as i32,

FILE: crates/libs/template/mgr/build.rs
================================================
fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../db/template-manager");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}

FILE: crates/libs/template/mgr-client/build.rs

fn main() -> anyhow::Result<()> {
    let manifest_dir = std::env::var("CARGO_MANIFEST_DIR")?;
    let proto_root = std::path::Path::new(&manifest_dir).join("../../../../proto");
    let proto_file = proto_root.join("template/manager/v1/manager.proto");
    let builder = tonic_prost_build::configure()
        .extern_path(
            ".space.sandbox.manager.v1",
            "::pplx_space_sbx_mgr_client::proto",
        )
        .extern_path(".space.net.policy.v1", "::pplx_space_net_common::proto")
        .extern_path(
            ".space.execution.v1",
            "::pplx_space_execution_common::proto",
        );
    pplx_space_common_proto_build::compile_protos(builder, &[proto_file], &[proto_root])?;
    Ok(())
}

fn main() {
    // sqlx::migrate! tracks existing files, but a newly added migration must
    // also invalidate the embedded schema.
    watch("../../../../../db/volume-manager");
    watch("../../../../../db/common");
}

// Cargo treats a missing watched path as changed on every build, so a wrong
// path fails here instead of silently rebuilding this crate forever.
fn watch(dir: &str) {
    assert!(
        std::path::Path::new(dir).is_dir(),
        "missing migration directory {dir}"
    );
    println!("cargo:rerun-if-changed={dir}");
}
